# Findings

This file records every review finding, bug report and known problem: what was found, and where it stands. A finding that exists only in a conversation gets lost, and a decision that is not written down gets made again.

- **Add a row when something is found**, before fixing it.
- **Status** is one of:
  - `open`
  - `fixed`: give the commit, by its hash or, for a row added in the same commit, its subject.
  - `not reproduced`: say what was tried.
  - `left as is`: say why.
  - `tabled`: say until when.
  - `planned`: give the phase in [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md).
- **Decisions the owner makes** are marked *(owner, date)*. Don't reopen them without a new fact.
- **Never put real names here.** The library is photographs of real people.

Findings fixed before 2026-09-23 are in the git log (`git log --grep="^fix"`). This file starts with what was still open or decided on that day.

| 29 | 2026-09-23 | Editing a caption renames a photo that Smart Rename named (one carrying PreservedFileName) by splitting its name on " - ": the first part is taken as the grouping and the second as the index. The name was built by a second copy of Smart Rename's code, which also misread every name made in a format other than the default. | fixed *(owner, 2026-09-23)*: *fix: a Smart Rename grouping cannot hold " - "*. Renaming moves forward, so the fix is at the grouping: a name already made that way is put right when Smart Rename next runs on its photos, and caption renaming is left as it is. It still misreads names made in a format other than the default, which the owner does not use. |
| 30 | 2026-09-24 | Renaming or deleting a tag in the tag tree, merging tags, and renaming a person rewrite each photo's whole keyword set starting from the index's copy of its tags, not the file's. The bulk writers were moved to the file for this reason (`tags_in_file`); this one was not. The servers build 36 error messages with an f-string. | obsolete: the servers are Flask (3b77587, 79cfe5e); an error's text goes in a UTF-8 body, never the status line (audit 2026-09-25) |

No tag in either library holds `_` or `%` today. | fixed: *fix: marking a branch marks only the tags under it*. `tagpup.store.taxonomy.sql_branch` matches the path and its `/`. |
| 37 | 2026-09-24 | Opening the tag tree first repairs it. It writes the repairs on its own connection, outside the write lock. Neither library has anything to repair today. | fixed: *refactor: the tag tree's edits are services*. `tagpup.store.taxonomy.repair` writes only when there is something to repair, and then through the lock. | 38 | 2026-09-24 | Five actions change a tag everywhere: renaming or deleting a node in TagPup's tree, merging or retiring a tag in TagTuner, and renaming a person in TagTuner. Each reaches a different set of places. Merging finds only photos carrying the tag itself, so a photo carrying only a tag under it keeps the old path, and the nodes under it stay in the tree. Renaming a person in TagTuner matches faces case-insensitively (`LOWER(name) = LOWER(?)`, which no index serves). It merges into the node if the new name already has one, where the tree's rename matches exactly and refuses. Only the tree's actions rewrite the library's taxonomy JSON. No two face names in either library differ only in case. | fixed: *fix: every way of changing a tag everywhere reaches the same places*. All five go through `tagpup.services.tags`. Merging and moving find the photos under the tag and move its branch in the tree, joining nodes already there. Renaming an unused tag keeps it under its new name. No tag goes under itself. Both person renames match any case without a function on the column. Every action keeps the JSON in step. Two differences are on purpose: the tree's rename refuses a path that is taken, and renaming a person joins it. Merging a tag leaves faces alone; renaming a person is where faces follow. |
| 39 | 2026-09-24 | Adding a tag and its missing ancestors to the tree is written five times: `TagTaxonomy.save_to_db`, twice in `seed_taxonomy_from_db`, `insert_tag_path_to_db`, and the create route. Each has its own copy of which new roots hold faces (#26). | fixed: *refactor: one writer of new tag-tree nodes*. `tagpup.store.taxonomy.add_path` writes every new node, with `vocabulary.FACE_ROOTS` as its rule, and a test keeps it the only writer. |
| 40 | 2026-09-24 | The Face Matching switch cannot be turned off for a root named People, Family, Friends or Pets. Turning it off clears the flag on the root and every node under it. Nothing in either library is under such a root without the flag today. | fixed: *fix: turning face matching off is not undone by the next tree read*. The read no longer repairs the flag. Every node is made with its parent's flag, so a node without it is one somebody switched off. |

| 44 | 2026-09-24 | Deleting, merging or renaming a tag counts a photo whose index row lagged its file as one it could not rewrite. A delete then answers that the tag was kept, and leaves the node in the tree though no photo carries it. A merge is not applied, and a rename warns that photos still carry the old tag. Found in review of phase 2; the service tests stood in for the whole rewrite and never met a skip. | fixed: *fix: a photo whose file already lost the tag is not one that could not be rewritten*, with tests that run the real rewrite. | 46 | 2026-09-24 | Reading a photo's keywords from its file (`tags_in_file`) says it raises when the file cannot be read, and does not when ExifTool cannot parse it: a file that is not an image (ExifTool calls it TXT) reads as having no tags. A tag node with nodes under it is refused unless the same change deletes them. | 187 | 2026-09-25 | Decision: retention is 90 days (`journal.RETENTION_DAYS`). A newer change is never pruned before an older one, so an undo's check of newer overlapping changes always sees their rows. | decided |

| 595 | 2026-10-03 | Phase 9d-1 third follow-up review: a resume re-shifts photos whose chunk the journal has not finished, because _settle_flight (tagpup/jobs/bulk_edits.py:654) relies on file_changes.settle either finishing the job's change or raising, and it does neither in two cases: (a) settle catches any exception from _finish, logs it and carries on (file_changes.py:701-706); (b) it skips a change whose owner is a live process (file_changes.py:697). The chunk's rows stay planned/writing (not done, not conflict), the resumed chunk's _write_fields settles first, writes the planned dates, then reads the shifted values and shifts them again. Demonstrated (a): crash after the 30th file is written and before it is recorded, _finish raises once at Resume: 21 photos at +180 min, job done, 0 errors. (b), no crash: the record of the 30th file fails after its write and release fails too; Resume in the same app leaves 1 photo at +180, reported done. | fixed: 2b12974 |

| 728 | 2026-10-04 | Search research (owner asked to verify FTS5 before 9e): comparable photo managers search typed words either by a derived word index (Apple Photos' psi.sqlite, WLPG, PhotoPrism's keywords table) or a substring scan (Lightroom, darktable, digiKam LIKE); structured filters are always joins; semantic search is a separate vector store (Immich VectorChord, LibrePhotos FAISS). Measured on photo_index read-only: LIKE scan 112-166 ms, FTS5 unicode61 0-0.8 ms (0.35 s build, 16.5 MB), trigram 0-1.1 ms (1.2 s, 36.8 MB), CLIP brute force over 68k x 1024 about 11 ms, the ViT-H-14 text encoder 0.3 s a query plus 14 s cold load on CPU. Decided: FTS5 in the library's database, two tables (unicode61 remove_diacritics 2 prefix '2 3' for words; trigram remove_diacritics 1 for file and folder names), contentless_delete=1, kept in step from the store's write points with one rebuild path and a doctor check (not per-row triggers); semantic search later, on the FAISS index Suggest already loads. Stored CLIP vectors are 1024 floats (ViT-H-14), not 512 as docs said. | decided |

| 918 | 2026-10-08 | Why nothing relinked a renamed folder: sync pairs a missing row with a new file only among the files its walk finds, and the walk is the roots and the folders the library holds; a renamed folder is a new name under the old parent, which no walk reaches (no root above it here). The watcher's watch on a folder sees nothing of the folder's own rename (the event is the parent's), and a watched folder that is gone is only noticed by its 30 s look, which synced whole when it came back, never when it went. Nothing was lost: rows were kept, reported as `folders_gone`. In the real library every row carries a DocumentID (1,309 of 1,309) and 1,305 a Date Taken, and the 8 folders were renamed by appending to their names, photo names unchanged; the 8 old names each begin with a date and the parent holds exactly one folder per date. | fixed: tagpup.services.folder_moves, `relink-folders` |
| 919 | 2026-10-08 | Design (owner, 2026-10-08): a folder gone from disk whose parent is there is the unit (the topmost gone folder); its candidates are the folders beside it whose name begins with the same date (a filter only); a candidate's files without a row are read for DocumentID and Date Taken; a file and a row are one photo by equal DocumentID (unequal rules a pair out), else equal size and equal Date Taken (or neither has one and the file name is equal); never a name alone. One to one across all candidates: a row with two matching files, or a file two rows match, is ambiguous and left. A unit is relinked when exactly one candidate holds matches for at least 80% of its rows with none ambiguous; otherwise proposed; a proposal the owner confirms with `--from`/`--to`. A file that already has a row is no destination (the 233 duplicate faces); rows of the unit that matched stay missing, never removed. The write is one journaled change, `relink_folders`, with `library.roots` and `library.ignored` entries under the old folder following in the same change. | decided: tagpup.services.folder_moves |

The design said "writes the files and records the ids"; recording first is what lets the identifier be stamped in the change's own transaction and a crash leave only a recorded id whose file the next run writes (`restore`, the recorded id) or, after an undo, a marker the next run adopts (`adopt`); no order leaves a second id. | 1019 | 2026-10-09 | DECISION: how a row says "read with the lens fields". A photo with no lens has no lens key either way, so "no Lens key in raw_metadata" cannot tell "read, has none" from "read before"; a re-read keyed on it would read the lensless photos again on every run (never idempotent, never resumable). Every full read (MetadataExtractor._structure, metadata.raw_metadata) now records `TagPup:ReadGeneration` (fields.READ_GENERATION, 2 = the lens fields are read) in raw_metadata; the re-read (tagpup.services.reread_fields) takes the rows whose generation is lower. No migration, no stamp of existing rows: only a row that is read gains it. A bulk write that merges into raw_metadata keeps whatever generation the row had. Not a column (a migration on the live libraries is the owner's choice); to be moved to a column if one is ever wanted. | left as is: decision for the main session to confirm |

Now LensID fields (Composite:LensID, LensID) are used only when they are not made of hex pairs or digits alone.
